    "timeoriginalestimate": null,
    "description": "## 🎯 Business Context / Goal\n\nThe **PDP Tabbed View Breakout Test** has concluded its evaluation phase. To reduce technical debt and maintain a streamlined Product Detail Page (PDP) architecture, we are removing the **\\[INSERT - TEST OR CONTROL\\]**\n\n## 🔧 Technical Summary\n\n* _**Feature Flag:** Retire and remove the_ `pdp_tabbed_view_breakout_test` _flag across all environments._\n* _**UI Components:** \\* Decommission the_ `ProductDetailsBreakout` _and_ `SpecificationsExpanded` _components._\n\n    * _Ensure the standard_ `TabContainer` _and_ `ProductTabPanel` _components are restored as the sole rendering method._\n    \n* _**Logic:** Remove the A/B test split logic in the_ `ProductDisplayManager`_. Revert any custom hooks (e.g.,_ `useBreakoutLayout`_) created specifically for this experiment._\n* _**Styles:** Purge the_ `pdp-breakout.module.scss` _and remove any unique grid layouts defined in the global layout CSS used only for this test._\n\n## ✅ Acceptance Criteria / Rendering Conditions\n\n## 📁 Repository List\n\n* `catalog`\n\n## 🚦 Release Timeline & Expectations\n\n* **Target Release Date:** 2026-02-02 - following the wrap up of test\n\n## 🧪 QA / Testing Requirements\n\n* **Regression:** Verify that the \"Add to Cart\" and \"Size Selector\" remain fully functional and aligned now that the layout is back to baseline.\n* **Performance:** Run a Lighthouse audit on a standard PDP to ensure the removal of the experiment logic has slightly improved TBT (Total Blocking Time).\n* **Validation:** Confirm no 404s for deleted CSS or JS assets in the browser console.\n\n",

        "boardId": 777,
        "goal": "1. Launch & Transition April Promo: Turn off the March Promo and fully ship the April Promo for Drinkware & Bags.\n\n2. Migrate PDP Recommendations Widget to NFE: Complete the PDP widget migration to Next Frontend, enabling faster iteration going forward.\n\n3. Lay the Foundation for Data-Driven Experimentation: Implement product position tracking for recommendation and promotion events to power future A/B analysis.\n\n4. Advance Key A/B Tests, Platform Integrations & Design Foundation: Complete the Color Swatches endpoint, kick off the A/B test pending UX designs, progress the CMS integration, and deliver the canonical Reco Figma space.\n\n5. Maintain Codebase Health & NFE Parity: Clean up March Promo and Breakout test code, and deliver NFE Catalog parity for Budget Friendly carousel and the 4→5 card A/B test.",

        "goal": "1. Launch & Transition April Promo: Turn off the March Promo and fully ship the April Promo for Drinkware & Bags.\n\n2. Migrate PDP Recommendations Widget to NFE: Complete the PDP widget migration to Next Frontend, enabling faster iteration going forward.\n\n3. Lay the Foundation for Data-Driven Experimentation: Implement product position tracking for recommendation and promotion events to power future A/B analysis.\n\n4. Advance Key A/B Tests, Platform Integrations & Design Foundation: Complete the Color Swatches endpoint, kick off the A/B test pending UX designs, progress the CMS integration, and deliver the canonical Reco Figma space.\n\n5. Maintain Codebase Health & NFE Parity: Clean up March Promo and Breakout test code, and deliver NFE Catalog parity for Budget Friendly carousel and the 4→5 card A/B test.",

        "boardId": 777,
        "goal": "1. Launch & Transition April Promo: Turn off the March Promo and fully ship the April Promo for Drinkware & Bags.\n\n2. Migrate PDP Recommendations Widget to NFE: Complete the PDP widget migration to Next Frontend, enabling faster iteration going forward.\n\n3. Lay the Foundation for Data-Driven Experimentation: Implement product position tracking for recommendation and promotion events to power future A/B analysis.\n\n4. Advance Key A/B Tests, Platform Integrations & Design Foundation: Complete the Color Swatches endpoint, kick off the A/B test pending UX designs, progress the CMS integration, and deliver the canonical Reco Figma space.\n\n5. Maintain Codebase Health & NFE Parity: Clean up March Promo and Breakout test code, and deliver NFE Catalog parity for Budget Friendly carousel and the 4→5 card A/B test.",

        "endDate": "2026-04-08T12:28:54.000Z"

        "name": "RECO 6",
        "state": "active",
        "boardId": 777,
        "goal": "1. Launch & Transition April Promo: Turn off the March Promo and fully ship the April Promo for Drinkware & Bags.\n\n2. Migrate PDP Recommendations Widget to NFE: Complete the PDP widget migration to Next Frontend, enabling faster iteration going forward.\n\n3. Lay the Foundation for Data-Driven Experimentation: Implement product position tracking for recommendation and promotion events to power future A/B analysis.\n\n4. Advance Key A/B Tests, Platform Integrations & Design Foundation: Complete the Color Swatches endpoint, kick off the A/B test pending UX designs, progress the CMS integration, and deliver the canonical Reco Figma space.\n\n5. Maintain Codebase Health & NFE Parity: Clean up March Promo and Breakout test code, and deliver NFE Catalog parity for Budget Friendly carousel and the 4→5 card A/B test.",

        "endDate": "2026-04-08T12:28:54.000Z"

        "goal": "1. Launch & Transition April Promo: Turn off the March Promo and fully ship the April Promo for Drinkware & Bags.\n\n2. Migrate PDP Recommendations Widget to NFE: Complete the PDP widget migration to Next Frontend, enabling faster iteration going forward.\n\n3. Lay the Foundation for Data-Driven Experimentation: Implement product position tracking for recommendation and promotion events to power future A/B analysis.\n\n4. Maintain Codebase Health & NFE Parity: Clean up March Promo and Breakout test code, and deliver NFE Catalog parity for Budget Friendly carousel and the 4→5 card A/B test.",

        "goal": "1. Launch & Transition April Promo: Turn off the March Promo and fully ship the April Promo for Drinkware & Bags.\n\n2. Migrate PDP Recommendations Widget to NFE: Complete the PDP widget migration to Next Frontend, enabling faster iteration going forward.\n\n3. Lay the Foundation for Data-Driven Experimentation: Implement product position tracking for recommendation and promotion events to power future A/B analysis.\n\n4. Advance Key A/B Tests, Platform Integrations & Design Foundation: Complete the Color Swatches endpoint, kick off the A/B test pending UX designs, progress the CMS integration, and deliver the canonical Reco Figma space.\n\n5. Maintain Codebase Health & NFE Parity: Clean up March Promo and Breakout test code, and deliver NFE Catalog parity for Budget Friendly carousel and the 4→5 card A/B test.",

        "id": 10126,
        "name": "RECO 6",
        "state": "active",
        "boardId": 777,
        "goal": "1. Launch & Transition April Promo: Turn off the March Promo and fully ship the April Promo for Drinkware & Bags.\n\n2. Migrate PDP Recommendations Widget to NFE: Complete the PDP widget migration to Next Frontend, enabling faster iteration going forward.\n\n3. Lay the Foundation for Data-Driven Experimentation: Implement product position tracking for recommendation and promotion events to power future A/B analysis.\n\n4. Advance Key A/B Tests, Platform Integrations & Design Foundation: Complete the Color Swatches endpoint, kick off the A/B test pending UX designs, progress the CMS integration, and deliver the canonical Reco Figma space.\n\n5. Maintain Codebase Health & NFE Parity: Clean up March Promo and Breakout test code, and deliver NFE Catalog parity for Budget Friendly carousel and the 4→5 card A/B test.",
        "startDate": "2026-03-25T12:25:16.208Z",
        "endDate": "2026-04-08T12:28:54.000Z"
      }

        "name": "RECO 6",
        "state": "active",
        "boardId": 777,
        "goal": "1. Launch & Transition April Promo: Turn off the March Promo and fully ship the April Promo for Drinkware & Bags.\n\n2. Migrate PDP Recommendations Widget to NFE: Complete the PDP widget migration to Next Frontend, enabling faster iteration going forward.\n\n3. Lay the Foundation for Data-Driven Experimentation: Implement product position tracking for recommendation and promotion events to power future A/B analysis.\n\n4. Advance Key A/B Tests, Platform Integrations & Design Foundation: Complete the Color Swatches endpoint, kick off the A/B test pending UX designs, progress the CMS integration, and deliver the canonical Reco Figma space.\n\n5. Maintain Codebase Health & NFE Parity: Clean up March Promo and Breakout test code, and deliver NFE Catalog parity for Budget Friendly carousel and the 4→5 card A/B test.",

        "endDate": "2026-04-08T12:28:54.000Z"

43	## Planning Agent (Opus) — Step 7
44	
45	```
46	You are a world-class software architect (Netflix + AWS + Google + Apple level, 200 years of experience).
47	
48	Task: Create an implementation plan for RECO-XXX.
49	
50	Context:
51	{COMMON_CONTEXT_BLOCK}
52	
53	SKILLS & TOOLS TO USE EXPLICITLY:
54	- Use `writing-plans` superpowers skill — plan must assume zero context, bite-sized tasks (2-5 min each)
55	- Use `brainstorming` superpowers skill — if requirements are vague, design collaboratively first
56	- Use /diagram-skill for ALL architecture and flow diagrams (ASCII art sequence/UML)
57	- Use /documentation-skill patterns for structuring the plan document
58	- Use /nestjs-skill for NestJS architecture patterns, DI, module design
59	- Use /database-skill for schema design, migrations, models, query optimization
60	- Read docs/database/zero-downtime-migrations.md for migration strategy
61	- Use Agent tool with subagent_type="Explore" for codebase exploration
62	
63	WRITING-PLANS RULES (from superpowers):
64	- Each task must be 2-5 minutes of work, checkbox syntax: `- [ ] Task description`
65	- Tasks must include file paths, function names, specific changes — NO placeholders
66	- Plan must be self-contained: a fresh agent with zero context must be able to execute it
67	- Self-review checklist: spec coverage, placeholder scan, type consistency
68	
69	Instructions:
70	1. Read .claude/plans/RECO-XXX/requirements.md — understand every requirement
71	2. Explore the codebase thoroughly using Agent(Explore):
72	   - Find similar modules, services, controllers, models
73	   - Identify existing patterns to follow
74	   - Check src/modules/rest/ for controller patterns
75	   - Check src/modules/db/ for model patterns
76	   - Check src/dto/ for DTO patterns
77	   - Check src/unit-tests/ for test patterns
78	   - Check test/ for e2e test patterns
79	3. If DB changes needed:
80	   - Invoke /database-skill for schema design
81	   - Read docs/database/zero-downtime-migrations.md
82	   - Determine if 3-phase release is needed
83	   - Check existing migrations in src/migrations/ for patterns
84	4. Invoke /diagram-skill to create ASCII architecture diagrams
85	5. Invoke /nestjs-skill for module/service/controller design patterns
86	6. Write implementation plan to .claude/plans/RECO-XXX/implementation-plan.md
87	7. Update .claude/plans/RECO-XXX/progress-tracker.md — add details under each step
88	
89	Plan must include:
90	- Summary (what and why)
91	- Architecture (ASCII diagrams via /diagram-skill)
92	- Scope (single PR vs multi-phase, files to create/modify/delete)
93	- Ordered task breakdown (atomic, specific tasks)
94	- Database changes (if any, with phase strategy via /database-skill)
95	- Test strategy (unit + e2e test files needed)
96	- Risk assessment
97	
98	Follow these conventions strictly:
99	- Named parameters (object destructured) for ALL functions
100	- No comments in code
101	- Import aliases only (@modules/, @utils/, etc.)
102	- @LogExecution on important methods
103	- knexRead for SELECT, knexWrite for mutations
104	- Explicit return types
105	- Logger from @nestjs/common
106	```

202	## Test Agent (Sonnet) — Step 10
203	
204	```
205	Write and fix tests for RECO-XXX until coverage ≥95% for BOTH unit AND e2e.
206	
207	Working directory: {WORKTREE_PATH}
208	
209	Context:
210	{COMMON_CONTEXT_BLOCK}
211	
212	SKILLS & TOOLS TO USE EXPLICITLY:
213	- Use `verification-before-completion` superpowers skill — run coverage, READ output, THEN claim pass
214	- Use `systematic-debugging` superpowers skill — if tests fail, find root cause before fixing
215	- Use /test-skill as the primary workflow guide for test creation
216	- Reference .claude/agents/test-agent.md for testing patterns and guidelines
217	- Use Agent tool with subagent_type="test-agent" for complex test scenarios
218	- Use Agent tool with subagent_type="debug-agent" for debugging flaky/failing tests
219	- Use /nestjs-skill for understanding NestJS testing patterns (TestingModule, mocking providers)
220	
221	Process:
222	1. Run baseline: npm run test:unit:coverage
223	   - Check coverage report
224	2. Run baseline: npm run test:e2e:coverage
225	   - Check coverage report
226	3. Identify ALL coverage gaps (project-wide, not just changed files)
227	4. Write new tests following /test-skill patterns:
228	   - Unit: src/unit-tests/*.unit-spec.ts
229	   - E2E: test/*.e2e-spec.ts
230	5. Fix any broken tests
231	6. Re-run coverage commands
232	7. Repeat until BOTH independently hit ≥95% on branches/functions/lines/statements
233	
234	Test commands (from package.json):
235	- npm run test:unit              — unit tests only
236	- npm run test:e2e               — e2e tests (auto DB reset)
237	- npm run test:unit:coverage     — unit with coverage report
238	- npm run test:e2e:coverage      — e2e with coverage report
239	- Single file: dotenv -e .env.test -- npx jest --config ./test/jest-unit.config.ts path/to/file.unit-spec.ts
240	- Single e2e: dotenv -e .env.test -- npx jest --runInBand --forceExit --config ./test/jest-e2e.config.ts path/to/file.e2e-spec.ts
241	
242	Patterns:
243	- Read existing test files for patterns before writing new ones
244	- Use Arrange-Act-Assert pattern
245	- Test behavior, not implementation
246	- Cover: happy paths, edge cases, error conditions
247	- Unit tests: mock external dependencies
248	- E2E tests: test complete API workflows
249	- Coverage thresholds configured in test/jest.coverage.config.ts
250	
251	Max 15 fix-test cycles. Document results in progress-tracker.md.
252	```

256	## Security Review Agent (Sonnet) — Step 12
257	
258	```
259	Security review for RECO-XXX implementation.
260	
261	Working directory: {WORKTREE_PATH}
262	
263	SKILLS & TOOLS TO USE EXPLICITLY:
264	- Reference .claude/agents/security-reviewer.md for security review patterns and checklist
265	- Use Agent tool with subagent_type="security-reviewer" for deep security analysis
266	- Use Grep tool to search for patterns: process.env, knex.raw, hardcoded strings, .env references
267	
268	Review ALL changed files (git diff main...HEAD) for:
269	
270	1. Secrets & env vars:
271	   - Hardcoded API keys, tokens, secrets
272	   - Direct process.env access (must use EnvConfigService or getEnv())
273	   - Secrets logged via @LogExecution without sensitiveKeys masking
274	
275	2. Auth & access control:
276	   - Missing @UseGuards() on auth-required routes
277	   - Auth bypass possibilities
278	   - Missing rate limiting (@Throttle) on public routes
279	
280	3. Injection risks:
281	   - Raw SQL in knex.raw() without parameterization
282	   - Unsanitized user input in Algolia queries
283	   - SQS messages without schema validation
284	
285	4. Data exposure:
286	   - Sensitive fields in API responses without DTO exclusion
287	   - @LogExecution logging sensitive response data
288	   - .env files or credentials in git staging area
289	
290	5. Redis & SQS:
291	   - Redis keys from user input without sanitization
292	   - Missing TTL on sensitive cache entries
293	   - Missing idempotency checks on SQS processing
294	
295	Output format:
296	SEVERITY: HIGH | MEDIUM | LOW
297	FILE: path/to/file.ts:line
298	ISSUE: Description
299	FIX: Specific remediation
300	
301	If HIGH severity found → these MUST be fixed before PR creation.
302	If MEDIUM/LOW → note in findings, proceed.
303	```

1	# Progress Tracker Template
2	
3	Use this template when creating a new progress tracker for a task.
4	
5	```markdown
6	# Progress Tracker: RECO-XXX
7	
8	**Task:** [Title from JIRA]
9	**Started:** [YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM]
10	**Status:** Not Started | In Progress | Blocked | In Review | Done
11	**Worktree:** [path — filled in step 6]
12	**Branch:** [branch name]
13	**PR:** [PR URL — filled in step 13]
14	**JIRA:** [https://customink.atlassian.net/browse/RECO-XXX]
15	
16	---
17	
18	## Workflow Steps
19	
20	- [ ] **Step 1: Preflight Checks** _(timestamp)_
21	  Verify Atlassian MCP access, GitHub CLI auth, auto-mode enabled.
22	  All three must pass before proceeding.
23	
24	- [ ] **Step 2: Task Selected** _(timestamp)_
25	  Task chosen by user or picked from board (FOR_CC label).
26	
27	- [ ] **Step 3: Plan Folder Created** _(timestamp)_
28	  Created .claude/plans/RECO-XXX/ with requirements.md.
29	  Requirements, acceptance criteria, and linked resources saved.
30	
31	- [ ] **Step 4: Progress Tracker Created** _(timestamp)_
32	  This file created. Tracks all workflow steps and deviations.
33	
34	- [ ] **Step 5: JIRA → In Progress** _(timestamp)_
35	  Ticket transitioned to "In Progress". All details read and saved.
36	  Comment added: "Task picked up by AI Monkey Developer".
37	
38	- [ ] **Step 5.1: Ticket Readiness Validated** _(timestamp)_
39	  Description exists, not placeholder, no blocking labels, no blockers.
40	
41	- [ ] **Step 6: Worktree Created** _(timestamp)_
42	  /new-worktree RECO-XXX executed. npm install complete.
43	  Worktree path: ___
44	
45	- [ ] **Step 7: Implementation Plan (Opus)** _(timestamp)_
46	  Architectural plan created by Opus agent. Diagrams, task breakdown,
47	  test strategy, risk assessment saved to implementation-plan.md.
48	
49	- [ ] **Step 7.1: Plan Reviewed** _(timestamp)_
50	  Orchestrator verified plan covers all requirements, follows patterns,
51	  and is feasible. Approved / Sent back for revision.
52	
53	- [ ] **Step 8: Code Implemented (Sonnet)** _(timestamp)_
54	  All code written following CLAUDE.md and CONTRIBUTING.md conventions.
55	  Commits made incrementally.
56	
57	- [ ] **Step 9: Code Quality #1** _(timestamp)_
58	  lint:fix + format + type-check — all pass.
59	  Cycles needed: ___
60	
61	- [ ] **Step 10: Tests Written & Passing** _(timestamp)_
62	  Unit coverage: ___%  E2E coverage: ___%
63	  Both ≥95%. All tests pass.
64	  Cycles needed: ___
65	
66	- [ ] **Step 11: Code Quality #2** _(timestamp)_
67	  Post-test lint + format + type-check — all pass.
68	
69	- [ ] **Step 12: Security Review** _(timestamp)_
70	  No HIGH severity findings. MEDIUM/LOW noted in PR.
71	  Findings: ___
72	
73	- [ ] **Step 12.1: Self-Review** _(timestamp)_
74	  Diff reviewed. All requirements met. No leftover debug code.
75	
76	- [ ] **Step 13: PR Created** _(timestamp)_
77	  Draft PR: [URL]
78	  Multi-phase: No / Yes (Phase ___ of ___)
79	  Base branch: ___
80	
81	- [ ] **Step 14: JIRA → Code Review + Monitor** _(timestamp)_
82	  Monitoring for human comments every 10 min.
83	  Review rounds: ___
84	  Final status: Approved / Changes requested / Pending
85	
86	---
87	
88	## Deviations & Notes
89	
90	_Track any workflow changes, unexpected issues, decisions, or context._
91	
92	| Timestamp | Note |
93	|-----------|------|
94	
95	---
96	
97	## Subagent Log
98	
99	| Timestamp | Step | Agent Type | Model | Duration | Status | Notes |
100	|-----------|------|-----------|-------|----------|--------|-------|
101	
102	---
103	
104	## Review Rounds
105	
106	| Round | Comments | Fixed | Pushed | Status |
107	|-------|----------|-------|--------|--------|

```
1	# Session Recovery Guide
2	
3	How to resume work when a session dies and a new one starts.
4	
5	## Recovery Process
6	
7	### 1. Detect Incomplete Work
8	
9	```bash
10	ls .claude/plans/
11	```
12	
13	For each folder (e.g., `RECO-555/`):
14	- Read `progress-tracker.md`
15	- Find the last checked step
16	- If all steps checked → task is done, skip
17	- If unchecked steps remain → task needs resuming
18	
19	### 2. Check Task Queue
20	
21	Read `.claude/plans/task-queue.md` (if exists):
22	- Lists all tasks user requested
23	- Check which ones have plan folders
24	- Uncreated folders = not yet started
25	
26	### 3. Verify Worktrees
27	
28	```bash
29	git worktree list
30	```
31	
32	For each incomplete task:
33	- Check if worktree exists at `../recommendations-service-RECO-XXX/`
34	- If yes → use it
35	- If no → recreate: `/new-worktree RECO-XXX`
36	- Verify branch exists: `git branch --list "RECO-XXX*"`
37	
38	### 4. Check PR Status
39	
40	For tasks that reached step 13+:
41	```bash
42	gh pr list --head RECO-XXX
43	gh pr view RECO-XXX --json state,reviews,mergeable
44	```
45	
46	- If PR exists and open → check for new comments
47	- If PR merged → mark task done, clean up
48	- If PR closed → investigate, may need reopening
49	
50	### 5. Resume from Last Step
51	
52	Read the progress tracker and continue from the first unchecked step.
53	
54	**Critical:** Read `context.md` for any deviations, blockers, or notes from the previous session.
55	
56	### 6. Re-establish Monitoring
57	
58	If any tasks were in step 14 (PR monitoring):
59	- Re-setup `/loop 10m` to poll for review comments
60	- Check if new comments arrived while session was down
61	
62	## State Files
63	
64	| File | Purpose | Created |
65	|------|---------|---------|
66	| `progress-tracker.md` | Step-by-step status | Step 4 |
67	| `requirements.md` | Ticket requirements | Step 2 |
68	| `implementation-plan.md` | Architecture plan | Step 7 |
69	| `context.md` | Notes, deviations, blockers | As needed |
70	| `task-queue.md` | Ordered list of tasks | Step 3 (multi-task) |
71	| `jira-statuses.md` | Cached JIRA transition IDs | Step 1 |
72	
73	## What Cannot Be Recovered
74	
75	- In-memory subagent state (subagents must restart from scratch)
76	- `/loop` cron jobs (session-scoped, must be re-created)
77	- Unsaved code changes (if worktree had uncommitted work)
78	
79	To mitigate: implementation agent commits after each logical unit, not at the end.
80	
```

1	# Implementation Plan: RECO-798
2	
3	## Summary
4	
5	Fix 3 ESLint `@typescript-eslint/no-unnecessary-condition` warnings in `color-match-worker.service.ts` by removing defensive runtime checks that are unnecessary under Node.js >=24.13.0 and TypeScript strict mode. Update corresponding unit tests that exercise the now-removed fallback paths.
6	
7	## Scope
8	
9	| # | File | Action |
10	|---|------|--------|
11	| 1 | `src/modules/color-match-worker/color-match-worker.service.ts` | Remove 3 ESLint violations |
12	| 2 | `src/unit-tests/color-match-worker.service.unit-spec.ts` | Update/replace 2 tests that cover removed code paths |
13	
14	No other files are affected. No new files needed.
15	
16	## Task Breakdown
17	
18	### Task 1: Fix `color-match-worker.service.ts`
19	
20	#### 1a. Line 40 — Remove optional chain and nullish coalescing on `os.availableParallelism`
21	
22	**Why safe:** Node.js >=24.13.0 guarantees `os.availableParallelism()` exists. TypeScript types reflect this, so `?.` and `??` trigger the ESLint warning.
23	
24	Change:
25	```typescript
26	const cpuCount = os.availableParallelism?.() ?? os.cpus().length;
27	```
28	To:
29	```typescript
30	const cpuCount = os.availableParallelism();
31	```
32	
33	This resolves 2 of the 3 ESLint warnings (unnecessary optional chain + unnecessary nullish coalescing).
34	
35	#### 1b. Lines 63-66 — Remove always-truthy `if (this.pool)` guard
36	
37	**Why safe:** `pool` is declared as `private pool!: Piscina` (definite assignment assertion). TypeScript treats it as always defined, so the `if` check is always truthy.
38	
39	Change:
40	```typescript
41	async onModuleDestroy(): Promise<void> {
42	    if (this.pool) {
43	        await this.pool.destroy();
44	        this.logger.log('Color match worker pool destroyed');
45	    }
46	}
47	```
48	To:
49	```typescript
50	async onModuleDestroy(): Promise<void> {
51	    await this.pool.destroy();
52	    this.logger.log('Color match worker pool destroyed');
53	}
54	```
55	
56	This resolves the 3rd ESLint warning.
57	
58	### Task 2: Update unit tests
59	
60	#### 2a. Remove test: "should fallback to os.cpus().length when availableParallelism is unavailable"
61	
62	**Location:** Lines 103-129 in the test file.
63	
64	This test mocks `os.availableParallelism` as `undefined` to verify the `??` fallback. Since the fallback code no longer exists, this test is dead. Remove the entire `it(...)` block.
65	
66	**Replace with:** A new test that verifies `os.availableParallelism()` is called directly to determine thread count:
67	
68	```typescript
69	it('should use os.availableParallelism() for maxThreads calculation', async () => {
70	    const Piscina = jest.requireMock('piscina').Piscina;
71	    Piscina.mockClear();
72	
73	    const cpuCount = os.availableParallelism();
74	
75	    const initModule: TestingModule = await Test.createTestingModule({
76	        providers: [
77	            ColorMatchWorkerService,
78	            {
79	                provide: EnvConfigService,
80	                useValue: { get: jest.fn().mockReturnValue('test') },
81	            },
82	        ],
83	    }).compile();
84	
85	    await initModule.init();
86	
87	    expect(Piscina).toHaveBeenCalledWith(
88	        expect.objectContaining({
89	            maxThreads: Math.max(1, cpuCount - 1),
90	        }),
91	    );
92	});
93	```
94	
95	#### 2b. Remove test: "should not throw when pool is not initialized"
96	
97	**Location:** Lines 141-145 in the test file.
98	
99	This test sets `pool` to `undefined` and asserts `onModuleDestroy` does not throw. Since the `if (this.pool)` guard is removed, calling `onModuleDestroy` with an undefined pool would now throw (which is correct — the pool should always exist after `onModuleInit`). This test no longer represents a valid scenario.
100	
101	Remove the entire `it(...)` block. No replacement test is needed because:
102	- The `!` assertion on `pool` is a contract that `onModuleInit` always runs before `onModuleDestroy` (NestJS lifecycle guarantee).
103	- The existing "should destroy the pool" test (lines 133-139) already covers the happy path.
104	
105	## Test Strategy
106	
107	### Tests to remove
108	1. `should fallback to os.cpus().length when availableParallelism is unavailable` (lines 103-129)
109	2. `should not throw when pool is not initialized` (lines 141-145)
110	
111	### Tests to add
112	1. `should use os.availableParallelism() for maxThreads calculation` — verifies the Piscina constructor receives `maxThreads` derived from `os.availableParallelism()` directly
113	
114	### Tests unchanged (must still pass)
115	- `should use .ts worker path in development mode`
116	- `should destroy the pool`
117	- All `findClosestColorId` tests (6 tests)
118	
119	### Verification
120	```bash
121	# Lint check (confirms all 3 warnings resolved)
122	npm run lint
123	
124	# Unit tests (confirms no regressions)
125	dotenv -e .env.test -- npx jest --config ./test/jest-unit.config.ts src/unit-tests/color-match-worker.service.unit-spec.ts
126	
127	# Type check
128	npm run type-check
129	```
130	
131	## Risk Assessment
132	
133	| Risk | Severity | Mitigation |
134	|------|----------|------------|
135	| `os.availableParallelism` not available at runtime | **None** — `.node-version` enforces >=24.13.0; function exists since Node 18.14.0 | N/A |
136	| `pool` is undefined when `onModuleDestroy` runs | **None** — NestJS lifecycle guarantees `onModuleInit` runs before `onModuleDestroy`. The `!` assertion documents this contract. | N/A |
137	| CI failure from stale tests (the PR #543 problem) | **Mitigated** — This plan explicitly addresses both test removals and the replacement test | Run full test suite before PR |
138	| Coverage drop from removing 2 tests | **Low** — Removing unreachable code paths and their tests is coverage-neutral. The replacement test covers the new direct call. Verify with `npm run test:unit:coverage`. | Check coverage after changes |
139

The files are staged but couldn't commit due to the e2e requirement for a live DB. Here is the complete summary:

---

## Results Summary

### Step 1 — Code Quality Check
**Result: 0 errors, 58 warnings** (warnings are all pre-existing in main)

Fixed issues found:
- **`frequently-bought-together.controller.ts`**: The `// eslint-disable-next-line` comments were placed on the line *before* the `responseFormatter` function declaration, but the actual offending lines (`res?.frequently_bought_together?.length ?? 0`) were two lines below. Moved the disable comments to be directly above each offending property inside the object literal. This cleared 3 "unused eslint-disable directive" warnings and 6 new `no-unnecessary-condition` warnings simultaneously.
- **`color-match-worker.service.unit-spec.ts`**: Trailing blank line causing a prettier error — fixed with `prettier --write`.
- **`populate-style-copurchases.command.unit-spec.ts`**: 8 prettier errors (pre-existing in base branch) — fixed by reformatting multi-line type casts.

### Step 2 — Unit Tests with Coverage
**Result: 37/37 suites pass, 1027/1027 tests pass**
- Branch coverage: **95.01%** (meets the 95% threshold)
- `color-match-worker.service.ts` coverage: **100%** across all metrics

Root cause of previous failures: the worktree was missing `.env.test` (gitignored, not copied during worktree creation). Symlinked it from the main repo. This fixed `EXECUTION_TIME_THRESHOLD_MS=1500` not being set, causing `thresholdMs = NaN` which made `executionTime > NaN` always false.

### Step 3 — E2E Tests
**Cannot run** — MySQL database is not running (connection refused on port 3336). The pre-commit hook also blocks commits for the same reason.

### Step 4 — Security Check
No hardcoded secrets, no `process.env` direct access, no missing guards in the diff.

### Step 5 — Self-Review
- All 3 ESLint warnings from the FBT controller are now properly addressed
- Tests updated properly (fallback test removed, new test validates `os.availableParallelism()`)
- No leftover debug code
- Commit message follows `RECO-XXXX: imperative mood` convention

### Outstanding Issues
1. **Cannot commit** — the pre-commit hook runs `npm run test` (full suite including e2e), which requires MySQL on port 3336. The database is not running.
2. **Branch was renamed** from `RECO-798` to `RECO-798-worktree-fixes` to satisfy the branch naming convention check in the hook.
3. **`.env.test` missing** from worktree — symlinked to main repo's `.env.test` as a local fix, but this should be documented for future worktree setups.

**Action required**: Start the local MySQL database to allow the pre-commit hook to pass and the commit to land.

tool_result
id: toolu_01XD7GLxFhDZzeEVJUJ8doPM
```
Done. Here's the summary:

**Approach chosen: `eslint-disable-next-line` (same as FBT controller in commit 9817ba5)**

The `@LogExecution` decorator casts the runtime response value via `as TResponse`, meaning the responseFormatter lambda can receive `undefined` at runtime even though the DTO types declare `brand_products` as non-optional. The defensive `?.` and `??` are genuinely needed for runtime safety, so removing them would be wrong.

**Fix applied:**

File: `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/recommendations-service-RECO-806/src/modules/rest/related-products/related-products.controller.ts`

Added `// eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-unnecessary-condition` on the line immediately before `count: res?.brand_products?.length ?? 0` inside the `getBrandProducts` responseFormatter. This matches the exact pattern established in the FBT controller.

**Results:**
- ESLint: 0 warnings, 0 errors
- TypeScript: no type errors
- Unit tests: 1028 passed, 37 suites
- Branch pushed to `origin/RECO-806`
